Ali, B. Jamieson, C. Bronner, D. Horiguchi, A. Minamino, Y. Sasaki

We report an indication of $\barν_e$ from the diffuse supernova neutrino background (DSNB) based on a spectral fit analysis of a 5003.0-day dataset in Super-Kamiokande. Key improvements over previous searches include enhanced rejection of atmospheric $ν$ backgrounds, updated evaluations of systematic uncertainties, and a refined spectral fitting method. Using a representative DSNB model, the best-fit flux was determined to be $3.6^{+1.6}_{-1.5}~\mathrm{cm^{-2}\,s^{-1}}$ for $E_ν>13.3$~MeV, corresponding to a $2.6σ$ tension with a null hypothesis ($99.5\%$ C.L., one-sided). Similar significances were obtained with signal models of varying spectral shapes.

Nonlinear Tides of Binary Neutron Stars

Guangzhou Guo, Zhen Pan

Nonlinear tides couple stellar oscillations and reshape the gravitational wave (GW) signal from inspiralling binaries. We present hydrodynamic simulations of nonlinear tides in inspiralling neutron-star binaries and, within a mode-coupling model, derive a semi-analytic quadrupolar fundamental mode-frequency shift through second order in the tidal strength. The two approaches agree in the perturbative regime, but diverge at stronger tidal fields, where perturbation theory breaks down. In the late inspiral, nonlinear tides reduce the fundamental-mode frequency by ${\sim}10\%$ for equal-mass binaries and add a GW phase shift of $\mathcal{O}(1)$ radian beyond the linear tidal response. These effects make nonlinear tides a promising target for next-generation GW detectors.

Gravitationally Induced Polarization-Angle Rotation in Pulsar-Black Hole Binaries

Jyotijwal Debnath, Amodio Carleo, Delphine Perrodin, Pratik Tarafdar

Radio pulsars emit highly polarized radiation whose polarization properties are commonly described by the Rotating Vector Model (RVM). In this work, we investigate the effects of gravitational light bending and parallel transport on the polarization profile of a pulsar in a hypothetical binary system with a black hole (BH) companion. We combine the RVM with numerical integration of null geodesics to calculate the polarization direction along the photon trajectory and model the orbital-phase-dependent variation of the observed polarization position angle (PPA). We consider both non-spinning and spinning black hole companions and include the evolution of the orbital geometry due to relativistic spin-orbit coupling. For a non-spinning black hole, the change in the observed polarization direction arises from the geometric shift associated with gravitational light bending. For a spinning black hole, frame dragging introduces an additional geometric shift, while parallel transport produces an additional polarization rotation measured relative to our chosen reference construction. We investigate the dependence of these polarization changes on the binary parameters and the orientation of the pulsar spin. We find that gravitational light bending can significantly distort the characteristic S-shaped PPA profile, particularly near superior conjunction, and can reverse its overall sense of variation for the supermassive black hole system considered here. Relativistic spin-orbit evolution also affects the orbital-phase dependence of the polarization profile, particularly in the supermassive black hole system. These results show that relativistic photon propagation can modify both the shape and the phase dependence of pulsar polarization profiles and may provide a useful probe of the geometry of pulsar-BH binaries when such systems are discovered.

Classification of Fermi Blazars of Uncertain Type Using Positive-Unlabelled Machine Learning

Sikandar Akbar, Zahir Shah, Zahoor Malik, Athar A. Dar

We apply positive-unlabelled (PU) machine learning to the 1623 blazars of uncertain type (BCUs) in the Fermi Large Area Telescope Fourth Source Catalogue, Data Release 4 (4FGL-DR4). Five PU classifiers are trained on seven gamma-ray features, once with the 820 flat-spectrum radio quasars (FSRQs) and once with the 1490 BL Lac objects as the labelled class (FSRQ mode and BL Lac mode). Each source is scored out of fold over 100 repeated five-fold splits, and its confidence is the fraction of splits in which at least three of the five classifiers flag it. At a confidence of at least 0.1, 437 BCUs are FSRQ-like, 597 are BL Lac-like, 12 are flagged in both modes and 577 in neither. On known sources the ensemble reaches a recall of 0.80 in FSRQ mode and 0.82 in BL Lac mode at a false-positive rate of 0.08, but a logistic regression trained on the labelled sources alone does at least as well, so PU learning gives no accuracy gain here. Removing the sky coordinates from the features does not lower the performance on known sources but adds 107 BCUs to the candidate lists and drops 30, which is consistent with the coordinates acting as a selection shortcut, so we exclude them. We check the candidates against published optical spectra, SDSS-V spectra, and BCUs that later received a class in the catalogue. Among sources assigned a single class, BL Lac-like calls are correct for 84 to 100% of the sources and FSRQ-like calls for 58 to 83%, depending on the sample. These samples come from spectroscopic follow-up and do not represent all BCUs. The candidates are fainter and lie closer to the Galactic plane than the labelled sources, and the excess of unclassified BCUs in the plane is not explained. We release the confidences of all 1623 BCUs.

An Intermediate Mass Stripped Star in a 4.2-hour Orbit around a Compact Object

Bethany Ludwig, Maria R. Drout, Ylva Götberg, Alexander Laroche, Katie Auchettl, Alejandro Vigna-Gómez, Katelyn Breivik, Cole Johnston, Eva Laplace, Vikram S. Dhillon, Hugues Sana

From their discovery as radio pulsars to landmark multi-messenger observations of their mergers, double neutron stars have transformed our understanding of the extreme Universe and the formation of heavy elements (Drout et al. 2017; Abbott et al. 2017). Yet, uncertainties in their formation result in predicted merger rates spanning orders of magnitude (Tauris et al. 2017; Mandel & Broekgaarden 2022). The most critical stage occurs when a star is stripped of its hydrogen-rich envelope, causing the binary orbit to shrink drastically. Despite its fundamental role, the resulting tight binaries comprising a stripped star and neutron star have remained elusive. Here we report a $\sim$$3.4\,M_{\odot}$ stripped star (Ludwig et al. 2026; Drout et al. 2023; Götberg et al. 2023) orbiting an unseen companion every 4.2 hours. Our orbital and spectroscopic analysis excludes a normal star, which would not fit within the orbit, as well as a second stripped star which would have been detectable in our spectra. Adding constraints from high cadence photometry gives a 99.4% probability that the companion is too massive to be a white dwarf, making a neutron star the most likely companion. We model the system's future and predict that the stripped star will transfer mass again before exploding as a hydrogen-free supernova, leaving a bound pair of neutron stars that will merge after 220 Myr. This system reveals a previously unconstrained phase of binary evolution, uncovering a new class of systems that probes the uncertain physics of massive stars, linking to observable gravitational-wave sources.

Revisiting supernova constraints on dark gauge bosons

Zun Wang, Yongchao Zhang

We revisit the supernova constraints on three well-motivated light gauge bosons: the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, and the $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ gauge boson. For the dark photon and the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, we re-evaluate the nucleon bremsstrahlung process within the one-pion exchange approximation, retaining the full momentum and mass dependence. The geometrical dependence of the decay and absorption of these gauge bosons inside the supernova core is also taken into account. Updated bounds on these models are derived based on the cooling of SN1987A and energy deposition constraints from low-energy supernovae (LESNe). Taking the SFHo-18.8 $18.8\,M_\odot$ profile, the SN1987A cooling constraints reach masses of about $230$ MeV, $280$ MeV, and $300$ MeV for the dark photon, the $U(1)_{B-L}$ boson, and the $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ boson, while the corresponding LESN limits extend to about $450$ MeV, $440$ MeV, and $600$ MeV, respectively. The kinetic mixing parameter $ε$ for dark photon, the gauge coupling $g_{BL}$ in the $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, and the gauge coupling $g_{μτ}$ for the $U(1)_{L_μ-L_τ}$ model are constrained down to $5.8\times10^{-12}$, $2.9\times10^{-16}$, and $6.4\times10^{-12}$, respectively.

Investigating supernova-remnant X-ray maps with dendrogram-based techniques

S. Calderaro, G. Parisi, V. Sapienza, E. Greco, G. Lo Bosco, R. N. Mantegna, G. Marsella, S. Miccichè, S. Orlando, M. Miceli

X-ray images of supernova remnants provide important information regarding the spatial distribution of the physical and chemical parameters of the shocked plasma. Our objective is to provide a comprehensive and quantitative assessment of the similarities observed across narrow-band images and equivalent-width maps of a given supernova remnant. We also aim to perform comparative analyses between distinct remnants. We adopted two dendrogram-based techniques, namely a multivariate analysis and a deep-learning, graph-based approach (by exploring different metrics) for the study of Chandra X-ray maps of Cassiopeia A, G292.0+1.8, Tycho's SNR, Kepler's SNR, and W49B. We also tested the reliability of our results on synthetic X-ray maps produced from a 3D hydrodynamic simulation of Cassiopeia A. We identify clusters of narrow-band images (and equivalent-width maps) corresponding to emission lines of chemical elements that are expected to be synthesized in the same layers of the exploding star and/or that originate from plasma at similar temperatures. By comparing different objects, we clearly observe strong similarities among Type Ia remnants (namely Tycho's SNR and Kepler's SNR) and core-collapse remnants (Cassiopeia A and G292.0+1.8), with W49B being more similar to the latter objects. The methodology developed in this work provides a robust tool to measure the analogies and differences between narrow-band images and equivalent-width maps of a supernova remnant, offering clues on the distribution of the different chemical species and on the plasma temperature. Although tested on a limited sample of objects, the adoption of this tool for the classification of supernova remnants (Type Ia vs. core-collapse) yields promising results.

The biases caused by interpolated table models and their impact on X-ray spectroscopy

Matteo Lucchini, Benjamin Ricketts, Phil Uttley, Daniela Huppenkothen, Fergus Baker, Gregoire Marcel, Guglielmo Mastroserio

Models for X-ray spectroscopy involving complex relativistic or plasma physics are commonly pre-computed on a grid, In this paper, we aim to quantify the potential biases and distortions introduced by the computational error and propagated into parameter inference by interpolating linearly these complex, non-linear models. We analyse the performance of Bayesian sampling algorithms on mock inference problems. Then, we model real observations of an accreting black hole X-ray binary in both the soft and hard states. We find that linear interpolation can a) greatly bias the posterior distribution of model parameters and b) distort the likelihood space to such a degree that posterior distributions become artificially multimodal. This multi-modality causes a noticeable loss in computational efficiency in both sampling and fitting techniques. As a result, exploring a given parameter space until convergence requires an extremely large computational cost. These findings call for improving the computational techniques used when fitting modern X-ray data, for example through better sampling algorithms and/or more sophisticated interpolation schemes

$Δ(1232)$ isobars and neutrino opacities in hot dense matter

Subha Mitra, Bharat Kumar

We calculated the diffusion mean free paths of electron neutrinos and antineutrinos in hot, neutrino-trapped matter containing $Δ(1232)$ isobars, as found in proto-neutron stars and neutron star merger remnants. We included charged-current absorption with the $NΔ$ weak vertex of weak pion production, neutral-current scattering on all baryons and three-body antineutrino absorption on electron--baryon pairs, with relativistic mean-field kinematics. The reaction $\barν_e+e^-+n\toΔ^-$ dominates the antineutrino opacity whenever the neutron and electron chemical potentials exceed the in-medium $Δ^-$ rest energy, even where the $Δ$ population is negligible. At two to four times saturation density, where the reaction is open, it shortens the antineutrino mean free path from \nubarLamNucMin--\nubarLamNucMax~m to \nubarLamDMin--\nubarLamDMax~cm. Because antineutrinos are scarce at high lepton fraction, this changes lepton-number diffusion only at low lepton fraction, by up to \DtwoRedHi\% at these densities and by a factor of \DtwoRedLow\ at 1.5 times saturation density. For electron neutrinos, absorption on the $Δ^-$ and, at low lepton fraction, the opening of nucleonic absorption shorten the mean free path by factors of up to \nuRatioYLthreeMax\ at lepton fraction 0.3 and up to about \nuRatioYLoneMax\ at lepton fraction 0.1. We also showed that, for a given axial coupling, the spin of the $Δ$ reduces its Gamow--Teller strength per particle from 3 to $5/3$.

Torque lags from magnetic unlocking in neutron stars and gravitational-wave smearing

Arthur G. Suvorov, Armen Sedrakian

In canonical twisted-torus models of hydromagnetic equilibrium in neutron stars, the toroidal field is confined to regions where the poloidal field lines are closed. As spindown torques applied at the stellar surface need not be communicated efficiently to such magnetically-unlocked regions, toroidal zones or magnetic voids may rotate faster than the electromagnetically braked part of the star. Continuous gravitational radiation from the associated non-corotating quadrupole may therefore be emitted at higher frequencies than poloidal ones to a degree that depends on the diffusive microphysics controlling angular momentum transport. Dissipative processes can suppress long-lived lags, and we estimate the relevant timescales and their sensitivity to microphysics. Nevertheless, we show that a quadrupole component whose pattern speed differs from the observed spin is shifted, or smeared, over a finite frequency band. Single-template or narrowband upper limits therefore constrain only the quadrupole contribution lying inside the searched frequency window, not necessarily the full frequency-integrated quadrupole. Using simple models, we quantify this spectral redistribution over finite observation windows and discuss its implications for continuous-wave searches.

Comparison of Hybrid and Standard Reconstruction Methods on IXPE Astrophysical Data

Riccardo Ferrazzoli, Nicolò Cibrario Stefano Silvestri, Michela Negro, Paolo Soffitta, Sudip Chakraborty, Simone Pagliarella, Steven R. Ehlert

The hybrid reconstruction algorithm developed by Cibrario et al. 2023 combined a convolutional neural network (CNN) with standard moment analysis to improve track reconstruction in IXPE Gas Pixel Detectors. Although validated on laboratory data (Cibrario et al. 2025), the method's astrophysical performance had not been systematically characterized. We present the first comparison of hybrid and standard reconstruction on IXPE point sources, measuring sensitivity gains versus energy. We analyze Her X-1, a bright, polarized accreting pulsar as a benchmark, and soft-state XTE J1701-461, a bright neutron-star low-mass X-ray binary with no detected polarization, to assess residual systematics. From both pipelines' Level-2 event files, we compare normalized Stokes parameters, polarization degree (PD) and angle (PA), and detection significance. For Her X-1, hybrid reconstruction increases significance at all energies, significantly above ~2.83 keV. R = σ_hyb/σ_std differs from unity by 9.3σ in the 2.83-4.00 keV bin and reaches 1.18\pm0.03 at 5.66-8.00 keV (6.2σ). For XTE J1701-461, PD is consistent with zero and R with unity. Fourier analysis reveals excess power in hybrid angle distributions at low energies for modes m = 3, 6, 12 and 15, significant at >7σ relative to the standard method; at 5-8 keV, all modes are consistent with noise in both methods. These CNN-amplified hexagonal-grid artifacts do not affect Stokes parameters because they are orthogonal to polarization modes.The method delivers a statistically significant sensitivity gain for Her X-1, consistent with Cibrario et al. 2025 and equivalent to ~20% more effective area without extra observing time. These results support systematic use of hybrid reconstruction on IXPE astrophysical data.

A frequency-resolved measurement of the scattering law in FRB 20210415C

Victor Pop, Mohammed A. Chamma, Aishwarya Kumar, Fereshteh Rajabi

Fast radio bursts (FRBs) show diverse temporal morphologies, shaped both by their intrinsic emission and by propagation effects such as residual dispersion and multipath scattering. Because several of these effects can broaden a burst asymmetrically, an asymmetric profile does not by itself establish multipath scattering. We measure the frequency dependence of the temporal tail in FRB 20210415C, a bright burst from the repeating source FRB 20210219C. The burst is detected across approximately 400-800 MHz. Frequency-resolved fits yield an empirical exponential-tail timescale, $τ_{\rm emp}$, that follows $τ_{\rm emp}\proptoν^α$. Two complementary treatments of the underlying pulse width give $α=-3.95\pm0.07$ and $-4.14\pm0.08$. Both results are consistent with the commonly adopted $ν^{-4}$ scattering law. The inferred scattering time is approximately 8.0 ms at 583 MHz, exceeding Galactic foreground estimates by a factor of at least $4\times10^{2}$. This favours a dominant contribution beyond the Milky Way, although the data do not localise the scattering screen. FRB 20210415C therefore provides a direct measurement of a scattering law commonly imposed in catalogue analyses.

EP251129a: A Fast X-ray Transient with Two X-Ray Plateaus and Its Off-axis Afterglow

Yue Wu, Ailing Wang, Senlin Pang, Yu-Han Yang, Biao Zhang, Zi-Gao Dai, Tao An, Jin-Jun Geng, Xue-Feng Wu, Hui Sun, Di Xiao, Jia Ren, Chen Deng, Yehao Cheng, Fa-Yin Wang, Yuan-Pei Yang, Eleonora Troja, Brendan O'Connor, Wen-Jie Zhang, Yuan Liu, Weimin Yuan, Yahui Jiang, Yun Wang, Cuiyuan Dai, Hucheng Ding, Tao Wu, Kangrui Ni

Extragalactic fast X-ray transients (EFXTs) detected by the Einstein Probe are extending the parameter space of relativistic stellar explosions toward softer and longer-lived transients. Here we report EP251129a/GRB 251129A at z=0.505, which exhibits two X-ray plateaus: an early plateau lasting $\sim 10^{4}\,\rm s$ followed by a rapid decay with spectral softening, and a second plateau extending to 60 days, placing it at the extreme long-duration end of Gamma Ray Burst (GRB) plateaus. The early optical emission evolves independently of the X-ray, indicating a different origin; whereas the unusual radio afterglow shows a rapid rise of $F_ν\propto t^{1.9-2.5}$ and a delayed peak at $\sim36-40$ days, providing strong evidence for an off-axis geometry. We find that the early X-ray behavior resembles those of ultra-long GRBs (ULGRBs) and can be naturally explained by the internal dissipation from a newborn millisecond magnetar undergoing gravitational-wave-dominated spin-down before collapsing into a black hole. The early optical, radio and late X-ray emission are well described by an off-axis two-component jet afterglow with additional energy injection, possibly powered by fallback accretion onto black hole. EP251129a therefore connects the emerging EFXT population with ULGRB-like central-engine activity and off-axis afterglow, offering a rare opportunity to probe both the evolution of central engine and jet structure in a single event.

Binary Progenitors of Electron-capture Supernovae

Liji Zhong, Yong Shao

Electron-capture supernovae (ECSNe) arise from a narrow progenitor parameter space between oxygen-neon white dwarf formation and iron-core collapse supernovae, governed by the competition between core growth and envelope removal. Binary interactions modify this balance through mass transfer and mergers. Using the binary population synthesis framework POSYDON, we model ECSN progenitors across metallicities from $Z/Z_\odot=2$ to $10^{-4}$, focusing on the initial parameter space, pre-supernova stripping, envelope-based classification, surviving companions, post-supernova orbits, and cosmic event rates. The lower edge of the binary ECSN initial primary mass lies near $7\,M_\odot$, owing to efficient convective overshooting in the POSYDON grids, whereas the upper edge increases with metallicity because stronger line-driven winds require larger initial masses to build carbon-oxygen cores within the ECSN range. Stable mass transfer and mergers occupy distinct regions in initial mass ratio and orbital period space. ECSNe in bound binaries are divided into hydrogen-rich Type II ($\sim30\%$), IIb ($\sim10\%$), and Ib ($\sim60\%$) outcomes according to residual hydrogen and helium envelopes. Across all channels, approximately one-third of ECSNe have a bound companion at explosion; the remaining two-thirds do not. Among these bound systems, approximately $90\%$ of the companions are main-sequence stars at explosion. These companions exhibit a bimodal spin distribution, including a rapidly rotating component associated with prior mass transfer. Evolved-star and compact-object companions each account for only a few percent. Combining the formation efficiencies with cosmic star formation and metallicity evolution models yields an intrinsic ECSN rate peaking at $z\sim2-3$ and declining at higher redshifts. Over cosmic history, ECSNe contribute approximately $1\%-4\%$ of all core-collapse supernovae.

Analytical modeling of the gamma-ray morphology of TeV halos around moving pulsars

Sarah Recchia

We aim to derive an analytical prescription for the line-of-sight-integrated particle distribution around moving pulsars, suitable for constructing spatial templates of the associated TeV-halo gamma-ray emission without resorting to computationally expensive numerical integrations. We consider electrons and positrons continuously injected by a moving source, undergoing isotropic diffusion and radiative energy losses. Assuming spatially uniform target photon fields, as appropriate for the cosmic microwave background and approximately valid for the interstellar radiation fields, the projected particle distribution provides a useful proxy for the inverse-Compton gamma-ray morphology. We show that the line-of-sight integration of the particle distribution can be performed exactly, reducing the calculation to a single numerical integral over the injection history. Under the additional assumptions of constant injection luminosity and a simplified treatment of energy losses, we derive a fully analytical expression that factorizes into an energy-dependent spectral term and a dimensionless spatial term describing the energy-dependent morphology. Comparison with the exact calculation shows good agreement over a broad range of energies and spatial scales, with appreciable deviations primarily at particle energies approaching the cutoff of the injection spectrum and at large distances from the source, where the emission becomes fainter. Our results enable the construction of physically motivated, energy-dependent spatial templates for gamma-ray halos around moving pulsars, capturing the morphological asymmetries induced by proper motion without computationally expensive numerical integrations. This makes them particularly suitable for systematic morphological and spectral analyses of extended gamma-ray sources.

Resolving a Clumpy Disk Wind in 4U 1957+115 with XRISM

Paul A. Draghis, Erin Kara, Ehud Behar, Jon M. Miller, Vera Berger, Joheen Chakraborty, Peter Kosec, Christos Panagiotou, Swati Ravi, Fatima Zaidouni

We present a high-resolution XRISM/Resolve observation of the persistent black hole candidate 4U 1957+115, revealing the first detection of an intrinsic disk wind in this source. The absorption is dominated by highly ionized Fe XXV and Fe XXVI and varies substantially throughout the observation, becoming prominent during the final $\sim40$ ks despite a modest decrease of only $\sim5$--$10\%$ in source flux. The observed evolution of the absorption cannot be explained solely by ionization changes driven by the modest continuum variability, favoring an inhomogeneous outflow with evolving line-of-sight properties. Time-resolved spectroscopy reveals substantially narrower absorption features in shorter intervals, with the inferred turbulent velocity decreasing from $\sim350~{\rm km/s}$ in the longer integration to $\lesssim70~{\rm km/s}$ in the shortest selections. This suggests that the broader absorption profiles measured over longer integrations may result, at least in part, from averaging over an evolving wind rather than from intrinsically larger turbulent velocities. Such time averaging can therefore affect the inferred physical properties of the wind and complicate efforts to identify its launching mechanism. Even though the wind carries only a small fraction of the radiative luminosity in kinetic form, its mass-loss rate may remain dynamically relevant. These results highlight the importance of time-resolved, high-resolution X-ray spectroscopy for characterizing the structure and variability of accretion disk winds.

Constraining Nuclear Matter from Finite Nuclei: the Ab Initio Perspective

Francesca Bonaiti, Sonia Bacca, Ingo Tews

This review surveys recent developments in ab initio calculations of finite nuclei aimed at constraining the nuclear matter equation of state. Particular emphasis is placed on observables that connect finite-nucleus structure to bulk properties of nuclear matter, including neutron-skin thicknesses, electric dipole polarizabilities, and monopole sum rules. We discuss how neutron skins and dipole polarizabilities provide information on the density dependence of the symmetry energy, while monopole observables constrain the incompressibility of nuclear matter.

ePNGCAT: the first eROSITA all-sky data release

Victor Doroshenko

The eROSITA telescope array aboard the joint German--Russian Spektrum-Roentgen-Gamma (SRG) mission delivered the deepest soft all-sky X-ray survey to date, scanning the entire sky more than four times between December 2019 and February 2022. The potential of the survey remains, however, hampered by the fact that only data for the half of the sky owned by the German consortium have been released publicly, whereas the other half remains accessible only to the Russian consortium, effectively making eROSITA a "half-sky" survey for most applications. The only publicly accessible all-sky "data product" as of 2026 is a single press-release image published in June 2020 jointly by both consortia to celebrate the completion of the first all-sky survey (eRASS1). Here I present the first all-sky eROSITA catalogue based on this image, containing around 63 thousand sources with an estimated completeness of about 50% above $3\times10^{-13}$ erg cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$ in the 0.2-2.3 keV band and a positional accuracy of $\sim$1 arcmin, validated against the full eRASS1 catalogue for half the sky and against several external catalogues. The catalogue is thus comparable with 2RXS, the deepest truly all-sky survey generally available, and gives a first glimpse of the eastern Galactic hemisphere with eROSITA. In this hemisphere, about 11000 detected sources have no counterpart in ROSAT or other public X-ray catalogues and are expected to be genuine new X-ray sources. Besides the catalogue, I release calibrated HEALPix maps to facilitate studies of large-scale extended emission, such as the eROSITA bubbles, or of Galactic absorption that require full sky coverage (https://zenodo.org/records/23640281), as well as the complete analysis pipeline to make the results fully reproducible (https://github.com/doroshv/eropngcat).

Contributions of massive stars and classical novae to Galactic 26Al

Manja L. Zimmerer, Thomas Siegert, Niklas C. Bauer, Tristan Bouchet, Patrik Ehrmann, Mika A. Gelowicz, Laura Eisenberger, Saurabh Mittal, Tomohiko Oka, Rudi Reinhardt, Manuel R. H. W. Skalka, Dimitris Tsatsis, Hiroki Yoneda, Arianna Vasini

The radioactive isotope 26Al is believed to be mainly produced by massive stars and ejected into the interstellar medium through their winds and supernovae. Due to its lifetime of 1.04 Myr, it traces recent nucleosynthesis and star formation. 60Fe is a similarly long-lived (lifetime 3.8 Myr) isotope, and only originating in ccSNe. Stellar evolution models in combination with Galactic Chemical Evolution (GCE) models tend to over-predict the 60Fe/26Al ratio. Population synthesis models that attempt to explain both, the 26Al mass in the Galaxy and its spatial distribution, only match the observational constraints with an enhanced star formation rate (SFR) of >5 Msun/yr. Classical novae may contribute to the 26Al production, potentially lowering the SFR inferred from massive stars and alleviating tension in the 60Fe/26Al ratio. However, previous studies suggest their contribution is negligible or < 10%. In this work we aim to determine the mass distribution of 26Al as a function of Galactocentric radius and estimate the contribution of (very) massive stars and classical novae to the 1.8 MeV signal. We are using 20 yr of INTEGRAL/SPI observations of the gamma-ray line at 1.8 MeV to test different assumptions on the radial structure of 26Al and compare the resulting mass distribution to different GCE models. For our baseline analysis, all tested source models prefer substantial nova contributions, with best-fit fractions of 77-84%. This alleviates tension in the 60Fe/26Al ratio and lowers the SFR from 26Al measurements accordingly to 1-2 Msun/yr. While it is difficult to gauge both contributions directly, next generation MeV telescopes can potentially identify individual massive stars, such as Gamma-2-Velorum.

Probing In-Medium Neutron-to-Dark-Fermion Conversion with Multimessenger Neutron Star Observations

Davood Rafiei Karkevandi, Mahboubeh Shahrbaf, Hamid Reza Moshfegh, Reza Karimi, Gordon Baym

Neutron stars provide a natural environment for probing in-medium neutron-to-dark-fermion conversion and its impact on the dense-matter equation of state (EOS). We use multimessenger neutron star observations to map excluded and not-excluded regions of dark-sector parameter space for an EOS ensemble within a non-linear $σ$--$ω$--$ρ$ relativistic mean-field (RMF) framework and determine their overlap across the ensemble. We include a fermionic dark component with repulsive self-interactions of strength $G_χ$. We consider masses $m_χ$ near the neutron mass and heavier states accessible in neutron star cores at sufficiently large neutron chemical potentials. To reduce sensitivity to the nucleonic EOS within this framework, we scan $m_χ=939$--$1500$ MeV and $G_χ=1$--$100$ fm$^2$ across 21 nucleonic EOSs: a main baseline set of 13 EOSs and soft and stiff limiting cases. Stellar sequences are tested against the requirement that the EOS support at least $2M_\odot$ neutron stars, GW170817 tidal-deformability bounds, and NICER mass--radius constraints, considering the Illinois--Maryland and X-PSI/Amsterdam inference sets separately. For the full ensemble of 21 EOSs, both NICER analyses identify similar regions excluded by all EOSs, with approximate minimum--maximum extents of $m_χ=939$--$1300$ MeV and $G_χ=1$--$11$ fm$^2$. Unlike the full 21-EOS ensemble, the main baseline set has a common not-excluded region whose extent depends on the NICER analysis. The soft and stiff limiting cases show how these regions shift when the nucleonic EOS predicts a maximum neutron star mass near $2M_\odot$ or large tidal deformabilities. Across these 21 calibrated nucleonic RMF EOSs, weak dark-sector repulsion is robustly disfavored across a broad dark-fermion mass range, while the extent of the remaining not-excluded region depends on the NICER analysis and baryonic EOS stiffness.

EP241021a as An Off-nuclear Month-duration Transient in a Dwarf Starburst Galaxy

Luming Sun, Xinwen Shu, Jiazheng Zhu, Ning Jiang, Bing Zhang, Jin-Hong Chen, Lixin Dai, Hucheng Ding, Chichuan Jin, Wenbin Lu, Rong-Feng Shen, Fan Xu, Lei Yang, Yunwei Yu, Weimin Yuan, Dongyue Li, Dong Xu, Tinggui Wang, Haonan Yang, Wenda Zhang

EP241021a is a peculiar fast X-ray transient exhibiting a long X-ray plateau and an optical rebrightening, both challenging the traditional jet afterglow model. Whether its nature is a stellar explosion or a tidal disruption event (TDE) remains debated. Here we present late-time HST/WFC3 and Gemini-N/GMOS observations of its host galaxy at $z=0.748$. After $\sim300$ days post-trigger, the transient emission had faded below detection, revealing a dwarf disk galaxy with an effective radius $R_e=1.7$ kpc, a stellar mass $M_\star\sim3\times10^8M_\odot$, and a high star formation rate of $\sim5\ M_\odot\ {\rm yr}^{-1}$. Combined with previous very long baseline interferometry measurements, the transient can be located in the galactic disk with a projected physical offset of $0.95\pm0.13$ kpc from the galaxy center. Host-subtracted optical light curves show that the rebrightening ceased at $\sim47$ days, followed by a sudden drop with a power-law index steeper than $-3.3$, coincident with a similar drop in the X-ray light curve. This synchronous drop in both bands is consistent with jet shut-off, and the required month-long jet activity suggests a persistently accreting compact object fed by a TDE. Among the TDE scenarios, the off-nuclear location and the starburst host galaxy slightly favor the post-supernova micro-TDE scenario in a binary system, in which a natal kick drives the newborn compact object into a tidal disruption of its companion. Nevertheless, an off-nuclear intermediate-mass black hole TDE or a stellar-mass black hole TDE induced by dynamical scattering in a dense stellar cluster cannot yet be excluded.

Experimental Evidence for Reflected Ions and Ion Temperature Anisotropy in a Forming Supercritical Magnetized Collisionless Shock

Ryo Yamazaki, T. Takezaki, T. Morita, S. Tomita, S. J. Tanaka, Y. Ohira, T. Umeda, K. Aihara, S. Kambayashi, R. Kawamura, K. Obayashi, M. Murakoshi, Y. Sato, R. Shiina, A. Takata, K. Matsui, S. Ueno, Y. Kidokoro, S. Yakura, Y. Okada, S. Suzuki, H. Kondo, N. Shimoda, J. Shiota, Y. Sudo, D. Suzuki, K. Sugiyama, A. Morita, H. Kawakatsu, A. Kashima, Y. Ishimura, K. Umehara, S. Egashira, H. Kanesada, K. Koba, T. Ogawa, R. Shiiba, T. Oguchi, M. Edamoto, M. Iwamoto, M. Hoshino, T. Inoue, S. Isayama, A. T. Ishii, Y. Kuramitsu, S. Matsukiyo, N. Ohnishi, K. Sakai, T. Sano, K. Tomita, Y. Sakawa

Supercritical magnetized collisionless shocks require ion reflection for dissipation, yet its role during formation remains unclear. Using high-power lasers, self-emission imaging, and collective Thomson scattering, we investigate a forming quasi-perpendicular shock. An upstream emission tail is consistent with a foot-like structure, while ion heating and temperature anisotropy extend upstream of a ramp-like region. These observations provide evidence of reflected-ion-related heating during early shock formation, although the incident and reflected ion populations are not separately resolved. Future measurements of ion distributions and plasma waves could clarify how this heating mediates shock dissipation and particle energization.

Identification of rippling interface between laser-ablated plasma and ambient uniform plasma at rest

Ryo Yamazaki, S. Tomita, T. Takezaki, T. Morita, S. J. Tanaka, Y. Ohira, T. Umeda, K. Aihara, S. Kambayashi, R. Kawamura, K. Obayashi, M. Murakoshi, Y. Sato, R. Shiina, A. Takata, K. Matsui, S. Ueno, Y. Kidokoro, S. Yakura, Y. Okada, S. Suzuki, H. Kondo, N. Shimoda, J. Shiota, Y. Sudo, D. Suzuki, K. Sugiyama, A. Morita, H. Kawakatsu, A. Kashima, Y. Ishimura, K. Umehara, S. Egashira, H. Ishihara, O. Kuramoto, Y. Matsumoto, K. Maeda, Y. Suzuki, M. Hanano, Y. Toyoda, K. Kuramoto, F. Nikaido, K. Iida, Y. Naito, Y. Sato, K. Takahashi, K. Nakahara, Y. Maenosono, Y. Muramoto, Y. Kanesada, K. Koba, T. Ogawa, R. Shiiba, T. Oguchi, M. Edamoto, M. Iwamoto, M. Hoshino, T. Inoue, S. Isayama, A. T. Ishii, Y. Kuramitsu, S. Matsukiyo, N. Ohnishi, K. Sakai, T. Sano, K. Tomita, Y. Sakawa

We present laboratory experiments on the early stage of the formation process of quasi-perpendicular supercritical magnetized collisionless shocks using laser-produced plasmas in an ambient uniform nitrogen (N) gas with 1~Torr under the external magnetic field with controlled polarity. An expanding aluminum (Al) piston plasma arising from laser irradiation interacts with a magnetized ambient N plasma, producing a bright, asymmetric self-emission whose global morphology reverses with the magnetic-field polarity. Two-dimensional ideal-magnetohydrodynamics simulations reproduce the main features of the observed structures and indicate that the ripple formation is unlikely to be driven by the Kelvin--Helmholtz instability, but is instead associated with vortex formation at the rounded leading edge of the piston plasma. We show that the bright, rippling emission delineates the Al-N interface, whose intrinsic width is consistent with an electron kinetic scale, and that its upstream side is predominantly composed of N plasma. This identification allows the formation of a supercritical collisionless shock in the magnetized N plasma to be discussed with minimal Al contamination.

Core-Collapse Supernovae from Single and Binary-Stripped Massive Stars - I. Explodability and Nucleosynthesis

J. M. Pollin, S. Curtis, C. Fröhlich, E. J. Griffith, A. P. Ji, D. H. Weinberg, J. A. Johnson, R. A. Patton, C. E. Fields

Binary interaction can substantially alter massive-star evolution and pre-supernova structure, changing core-collapse outcomes and nucleosynthetic yields. We compare explodability and nucleosynthesis in 66 non-rotating, solar-metallicity single-star and binary-stripped progenitors spanning zero-age main-sequence (ZAMS) masses of $11$-$45\,{\rm M}_\odot$, using the spherically symmetric PUSH framework and nucleosynthesis post-processing. Both populations explode up to $22\,{\rm M}_\odot$, with binary stripping altering explodability at higher ZAMS masses. Successful explosions span similar energies ($\sim$0.4-1.3$\,$B) and remnant masses ($\sim$1.4-2.0$\,{\rm M}_\odot$). The largest nucleosynthetic differences occur at low ZAMS masses, particularly for material undergoing explosive O and Si burning. The $^{56}$Ni yields and iron-group abundances broadly agree with observations of core-collapse supernovae and the metal-poor star HD 84937. When compared with calculations using time-dependent mixing-length theory and a two-parameter explodability criterion for the same progenitors, our results reveal substantial differences in explodability, with agreement on successful explosions for only 5 cases across all three approaches. Nevertheless, elemental yield patterns can remain broadly similar for models that explode successfully across treatments. Salpeter initial-mass-function-weighted yields of most elements differ by $\sim$20% between populations, with K enhanced by a factor of $\sim$3.4 in the binary-stripped population, driven by increased production in the lowest-mass progenitors. Binary-stripped models show closer agreement with empirical core-collapse S, K, and Ca abundance ratios relative to Mg inferred from APOGEE, while Na remains overproduced in both populations. Together, our results highlight the need to account for binarity when interpreting supernova abundance patterns.

Finite-temperature ab-initio-Informed Nuclear Equation-of-State Tables (FINEST): Nuclear matter uncertainties in core-collapse supernovae

Finia P. Jost, André da Silva Schneider, Almudena Arcones, Achim Schwenk, Evan O'Connor, Gerard Navó, Yeunhwan Lim

We construct new finite-temperature, composition-dependent equation of state (EOS) tables for astrophysical simulations by implementing a recently developed energy-density functional. The functional is constrained by ab initio nuclear matter calculations and neutron-star observations. A distinguishing feature of this functional is a non-monotonic density dependence of the nucleon effective mass, with an increase beyond saturation density. Using these EOS tables, we investigate the impact of current uncertainties in nuclear matter properties on core-collapse supernova (CCSN) evolution with spherically symmetric simulations employing an effective treatment of convective mixing. The magnitude of the EOS effects depends on the progenitor compactness. Within current constraints, variations in incompressibility, symmetry energy, and high-density pressure produce only modest differences during the early post-bounce evolution. In contrast, the effective mass dependence has a much stronger influence on proto-neutron star contraction and the explosion dynamics in a stellar model with high core compactness. Its non-monotonic density dependence leads to a qualitatively different contraction history and can alter the explosion outcome compared to mean-field effective-mass behaviors. Therefore, among the nuclear matter properties considered, the density dependence of the nucleon effective masses has by far the largest impact on CCSN dynamics. This highlights the importance of realistic effective-mass parameterizations in nuclear EOSs used in astrophysical simulations.

Magnetoelastic avalanches in magnetar crusts with plastic islands

Arthur G. Suvorov

High-energy outbursts from magnetars may be associated with crustal failures or quakes. The magnetoelastic modes of the star can be excited to large amplitudes in such a framework, providing a natural explanation for the quasi-periodic oscillations observed in the X-ray tails of some events. However, previous calculations of torsional modes have been carried out assuming an intact crust without accounting for the failure itself. We develop a code in this paper that solves the axial sector of the linearised Einstein-Maxwell-Euler equations in a cavity that hosts inelastic regions or 'plastic islands'. As crustal elements are displaced, shear stresses are tracked as a function of time to allow cells to dynamically switch from elastic to inelastic states. The presence of islands in the crust promotes phase mixing and, when reflected waves mix constructively, large sections fail collectively ('avalanche') if the initial kick is strong enough. Transient spectral features are observed as a result of the progressive loss of elastic support, which may explain the temperamental nature of the oscillations observed following giant flares and magnetar storms.

Far Ultraviolet Variability on Timescales of a Few hours in the Recurrent Nova M31N 2008-12a: Origin and Implications

Judhajeet Basu, Sudhanshu Barway, G. C. Anupama, Shatakshi Chamoli, Kulinder Pal Singh, Varun Bhalerao, M. Pavana, Vishwajeet Swain

The fate of the accretion disk during nova eruptions remains an open question, particularly in rapidly recurring novae, where high accretion rates and low ejecta masses may allow the disk to survive the eruption. M31N 2008-12a, the most rapidly recurring nova currently known, provides an ideal laboratory for investigating this process. We present high-cadence AstroSat/UVIT observations obtained during eruptions between 2019 and 2023. Complementary multiwavelength observations of the 2023-2025 eruptions are also presented, which are broadly consistent with prior outbursts. The main result is the early onset of intrinsic far-ultraviolet variability on a timescale of a few hours, approximately 4-5 days after eruption, which is of astrophysical origin. A periodicity search suggests a tentative signal at 4.60 (+0.25,-0.15) hr at 2.3 sigma confidence, detected in high-cadence UVIT/FUV observations. We show that a Keplerian orbit of this timescale lies not only within the Roche lobe of the white dwarf in M31N 2008-12a, but also within the circularisation radius. A disk-related origin is, thus, physically plausible. The presence of this UV variability so soon after eruption suggests that accretion structures may already exist during the early post-eruption phase, supporting the disk-survival hypotheses. If confirmed by future observations, this method may provide a new dynamical probe of accretion disk survival in rapidly recurring novae.

Effects of Synchrotron Self-Absorption on Polarization Diagnostics of MHD Turbulence

Ning-Ning Guo, Jian-Fu Zhang, Hua-Ping Xiao, Qing-Zhong Liu

Synchrotron polarization is a powerful tool for diagnosing magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) turbulence, yet prior analyses are confined to the optically thin regime, neglecting the effects of synchrotron self-absorption (SSA). Here, we incorporate SSA into the radiative transfer of polarized synchrotron emission and employ 3D MHD turbulence simulations to investigate its impacts on polarization statistics. We show that SSA strongly modulates polarized intensity by suppressing its emission amplitude, steepening its low-frequency power-law slopes, and reducing scale-dependent anisotropy. Notably, SSA does not impede the recovery of the scaling slope of the magnetic turbulence. Our results extend the applicability of synchrotron polarization diagnostics to optically thick astrophysical environments, providing a theoretical foundation for interpreting multi-frequency radio polarization observations. This confirms that polarization statistical techniques remain effective for diagnosing MHD turbulence properties in astrophysical environments where SSA is present.

PeV Diffuse Neutrinos from Magnetically Arrested Disks in Low-Luminosity Active Galactic Nuclei

Fan Wu, Benzhong Dai

Radiatively inefficient accretion flows (RIAFs) in low-luminosity active galactic nuclei (LLAGNs) are promising candidates for the PeV astrophysical diffuse neutrino background (DNB), but how the magnetically arrested disk (MAD) state affects their neutrino output and what fraction of LLAGNs are in this state remain unclear. We present a comprehensive study of diffuse neutrino emission from the inner accretion flows of LLAGNs in the MAD state, including all three flavors and both hadronuclear ($pp$) and photohadronic ($pγ$) production channels. Assuming that $\sim 5\%$ of the accretion power is transferred to nonthermal protons, current IceCube data allow at most $2.3\times10^{-3}$ of LLAGNs to have their inner accretion flows in a MAD state that can produce high-energy neutrinos. A lower fraction of the accretion power transferred to nonthermal protons would allow a proportionally larger fraction of such LLAGNs. Assuming that the comoving number density of low-excitation radio galaxies (LERGs) approximately matches that of LLAGNs in the MAD state, we also estimate a MAD fraction of $\simeq 1.6\times10^{-3}$. The corresponding diffuse intensity is compatible with current IceCube data and testable by IceCube-Gen2. For this MAD fraction, the all-flavor diffuse intensity peaks at $E_ν\simeq1.5\,{\rm PeV}$ with $E_ν^2Φ_ν\simeq2.6\times10^{-9}\,{\rm GeV\,cm^{-2}\,s^{-1}\,sr^{-1}}$. Current IceCube data are beginning to challenge several predictions from previous LLAGN RIAF models, and the MAD component can become the dominant contribution from LLAGN accretion flows beyond the cutoffs of most earlier RIAF spectra considered here.%%%This constraint suggests that only a small fraction of LLAGNs are supplied with sufficient coherent net poloidal magnetic flux.

Fast Outflow Driven from the Magnetically Arrested Disc

Taili Yi, Jia-Wen Li

Fast outflows are commonly observed in accreting black hole systems, yet their launching mechanism remains debated, especially when the gas is highly ionized and radiation line driving becomes inefficient. We investigate magnetically driven outflows from magnetically arrested discs (MADs) using a steady, axisymmetric magnetocentrifugal outflow model. The outflow equations follow the classical trans-magnetosonic MHD framework, while the disc structural scalings and outflow-base conditions are motivated by general relativistic magnetohydrodynamics (GRMHD) simulations. We solve for the trans-magnetosonic outflow solutions along prescribed large-scale poloidal magnetic field lines. The outflows launched from the inner disc are strongly accelerated and the formal Newtonian solutions can enter the mildly relativistic regime. The outflow velocity decreases systematically with increasing footpoint radius, producing a radially stratified outflow in which inner streamlines reach UFO-like velocities while outer streamlines form slower components. We also find that the MAD solutions have a large magnetic lever arm $\tilde{l}=(R_{\rm A}/R)^2$ is typically several tens and can reach $\sim10^2$, which implies that the outflow can carry a large amount of the specific angular momentum of the disc material at its footpoint, and we find that about $55\%$ of the angular-momentum estimated between the outer and inner disc boundaries is removed by the magnetic outflow. The total mass-loss rate in the outflow is about $14\%$ of the disc mass-accretion rate in our fiducial model. These results suggest that the MADs provide a natural environment for producing both fast, radially stratified disc outflow and efficient disc angular-momentum removal. MAD-driven outflow may therefore provide a magnetic origin for ultra-fast absorbers in low-luminosity active galactic nuclei (AGNs) and X-ray binaries (XRBs).

Gravitational Waves from Magnetically Confined Mountains on Accreting White Dwarfs

Pedro Henrique Barboza Rossetto, Manoel Felipe Sousa, Diego Antonio Falceta Gonçalves

We propose fast-spinning, magnetic white dwarfs in binary systems as promising sources of continuous gravitational waves for future space-based detectors. The aim of this work is to determine the maximum ellipticities and gravitational wave amplitudes achievable by magnetically confined mountains accreted on the star's magnetic poles, and to assess their detectability against predicted sensitivity curves. Self-consistent magnetohydrodynamic equilibrium simulations are used to characterize the structure of the accreted mountain. A parametric search is then performed on these simulations to determine the maximum mass values that can be confined by the star's magnetic field. The stellar ellipticity $\varepsilon$ grows nearly linearly with accreted mass $M_{\rm a}$ up to a maximum, and was found to scale as $\varepsilon_{\rm max} \simeq 1.6\times10^{-2}T_5^{-2} B_{*,8}^{2}$, while the maximum gravitational wave amplitude as $h_{\rm 0, max} \simeq 1.2\times10^{-23}T_5^{-2} B_{*,8}^{2}$, where $T_5$ represents the temperature of the mountain in units of $10^5 \mathrm{K}$ and $B_{*,8}$ represents the star's polar magnetic field intensity in units of $10^8 \mathrm{G}$. Across a representative range of source inclinations, distances and spin periods, magnetically confined mountains on white dwarfs can generate continuous gravitational waves detectable by future space-based interferometers. BBO and DECIGO can probe diverse configurations, whereas LISA, TianQin, and Taiji require highly magnetized systems. Applications to known fast-spinning white dwarfs indicate that cooler accreted mountains favor detectability.

Late Epoch Infrared Spectroscopy and Two-Component 3D Photoionization Modeling of Classical Nova V5668 Sgr

Sarah H. Taft, Charles E. Woodward, Bruno C. Santos, D. P. K. Banerjee, R. D. Gehrz

V5668 Sgr is an Fe II classical nova (CN) that exploded on 15 March 2015. We present near-infrared (NIR) spectroscopy of the ejecta from the Mt. Abu observatory which exhibits emission lines of species H, He, N, S, Ti, and Si. Accretion Geometry and Variability in QS Telescopii from multi-epoch Observations

L Booi, S. B. Potter, Z. N. Khangale, V Moloi, P. A. Woudt

We present phase-resolved photometry, spectroscopy, photopolarimetry, and circular spectropolarimetry of the polar QS Telescopii, obtained between 2017 and 2025 using the South African Astronomical Observatory (SAAO) 1.0-m and 1.9-m telescopes, the Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS), Asteroid Terrestrial-impact Last Alert System (ATLAS) and the Southern African Large Telescope (SALT). Neutron Star--Primordial Black Hole Binaries: Dynamical Tides, Resonance Locking, and Gravitational Waves

Isaac Legred, Nicolás Yunes

The orbital evolution of a neutron star and a much lower-mass primordial black hole can differ strikingly from that of typical compact binaries. Dynamical tides, analogous to those in the Solar System's giant planet-moon systems, can accelerate inspiral or induce outspiral, depending on the orbital configuration. Initial Conditions of Magnetized AGN Accretion Disks from Cloud Capture and Disruption

Philip F. Hopkins

Accretion onto supermassive black holes (BHs) from the ISM can occur via capture of cold gas 'clouds' (GMCs, clumps, or overdense structures). Unlike material in stellar tidal disruptions or X-ray binaries, or classical Bondi accretion, ISM clouds are strongly magnetized (plasma $β\equiv P_{\rm thermal}/P_{\rm magnetic} \ll 1$), with supersonic turbulence, and cool rapidly. We derive analytic scalings for the initial conditions of accretion disks formed by capture/disruption of such clouds, in three regimes: (1) low-mass BHs ($M_{\rm BH} \ll M_{\rm cl}$), where turbulent Bondi-Hoyle or hierarchical 'leapfrog' growth via sub-cloud encounters dominates; (2) comparable-mass encounters ($M_{\rm BH} \sim M_{\rm cl}$); and (3) massive BHs tidally disrupting small clouds ($M_{\rm BH} \gg M_{\rm cl}$). In each, the resulting disk is predicted to be initially toroidally-magnetically-dominated ($|B_φ| \gg |B_{z}|$, $β\ll 1$), geometrically thick ($H/R \sim 0.1-1$), and gravitationally stable ($Q \gtrsim 1$), with super-sonic, trans-Alfvenic turbulence. These conditions differ qualitatively from those assumed in classical accretion/$α$ disk theory, 'chaotic cold accretion', stellar tidal disruption, or Bondi-Hoyle accretion. At least initially, the toroidal field should be sustained by flux-freezing on timescales shorter than Parker or turbulent resistive losses. We examine how cloud mass spectra drive accretion rate distributions, duty cycles, and fueling rates, with integrated BH growth dominated by clouds of mass comparable to the BH. Given known cosmological evolution, we suggest a scenario linking (a) inefficient growth and wandering BHs in dwarf galaxies, (b) hyper-Eddington growth in little red dots, (c) peak quasar activity in massive gas-rich systems at cosmic noon, and (d) lower-luminosity AGN in gas-poor galaxies at late times.

V. Allingham, Ricardo O. Amorín, Franz E. Bauer, Larry Bradley, Mateusz Bronikowski, Christopher J. Conselice, David A. Coulter, James M. DerKacy, José M. Diego, Eiichi Egami, Andreas L. Faisst, Ori D. Fox, Brenda L. Frye, Yoshinobu Fudamoto, Mauro González-Otero, Massimo Griggio, Nimish P. Hathi, Tiger Yu-Yang Hsiao, Saurabh W. Jha, Y. Jiménez-Teja, Jeyhan S. Kartaltepe, Gourav Khullar, Zachary G. Lane, Paulo A. A. Lopes, Ray A. Lucas, Georgios E. Magdis, Matteo Messa, Rohan P. We present the discovery of supernova (SN) Ares, a strongly lensed supernova at $z_{\rm spec}=1.276\pm0.002$ in a massive, triply imaged host galaxy behind the MACS J0308.9+2645 galaxy cluster. $El\ Ascua$: An ultra-luminous, radio-loud, Compton-thick AGN at $z\sim 3.8$ hiding in plain sight revealed by MINERVA multi-band MIRI observations

Aidan P. Cloonan, Edgar P. Vidal, Alexandra Pope, Anna Sajina, Nicholas S. Martis, Yoshihisa Asada, Olivia R. Cooper, Adam Muzzin, Stacey Alberts, Jacqueline Antwi-Danso, Gabriel Brammer, Sam E. Cutler, Kartheik G. Iyer, Ivo Labbé, Danilo Marchesini, Tim B. Miller, Ikki Mitsuhashi, Luke Robbins, Ghassan T. E. Sarrouh, Monu Sharma, Mauro Stefanon, Katherine A. Suess, Katherine E. Whitaker, Chris J. Willott, Pratika Dayal, Anna de Graaff, Ben Forrest, Karl Glazebrook, Jenny E. Greene, Kelsey Hargraves, William W. H. Jarvis, Daniel J. Lyon, Ian McConachie, Gaël Noirot, Veronica Pratt, Bingjie Wang

Heavily dust-obscured active galactic nuclei (AGN) are crucial for understanding supermassive black hole growth. Using multi-band MIRI photometry from the MINERVA survey, we report the discovery in the UDS field of an extremely mid-infrared bright Compton-thick AGN (CT AGN) at $z \sim 3.8$, \textit{el Ascua} (the Ember). The AGN and/or its host galaxy is detected at 870\,$μ$m with ALMA and at 1.4\,GHz with the VLA. With UV-to-radio spectral energy distribution modeling, we estimate that the dusty host galaxy has a stellar mass of $\log\left( M_\star / M_\odot \right)=10.82 \pm 0.57$ and a star formation rate of ${\rm{SFR}} =157 \pm 8 \,M_\odot\,{\rm yr^{-1}}$, and we confirm the presence of a powerful dusty AGN with an intrinsic accretion luminosity of $\log\left( L_{\rm{acc}} /{\rm erg\,s^{-1}} \right)=47.12 \pm 0.58$. CT obscuration is revealed by comparing the upper limit on the X-ray luminosity over $2-10 \,{\rm{keV}}$ with the $6 \,{\rmμ{m}}$ mid-IR AGN luminosity. We additionally find evidence for a radio excess by comparing its total IR-to-radio ratio to those of typical star-forming galaxies and radio-quiet AGN, implying that \textit{el Ascua} could be a rare radio-loud CT AGN. With an extremely high ratio between black hole accretion rate and SFR, the AGN is likely nearing the peak of a deeply obscured phase of black hole growth before AGN feedback blows away the obscuring material.
